\displaystyle(\lambda_{i}w_{n},\ldots,\lambda_{i}w_{i},w_{i-1},\ldots,w_{0})\ .

The action (
17.9
) restricts to an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
on
W
. Furthermore, let
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
act on
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}
componentwise via the map

\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
given by
(\mu,\lambda)\mapsto\lambda\mu^{-1}
, where
\mu^{-1}
is the inverse in the group
\mathbb{G}_{m}
.
We deduce that (
17.9
) is equivariant for the diagonal action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
on
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W
, in other words, the following diagram commutes

(17.11)

\begin{array}[]{ccc}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\times(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W)&\longrightarrow&\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow_{m}\\
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W&\overset{m}{\longrightarrow}&W\end{array}

where the vertical map on the left is projection onto
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W
, and the horizontal map is the diagonal action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
on both
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}
and
W
.
In the case
n=1
this diagram merely expresses the trivial fact that

m(\mu.(\lambda,w))=m(\lambda\mu^{-1},\mu w)=\lambda w=m(\lambda,w)
.

17.3.3.
Quotients and blowups

For any finite dimensional vector space
W
over
k
, let
W^{\times}=W\backslash\{0\}
. It is an open subscheme of
W
stable under the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, but not under the extended action of
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, since
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times W^{\times}\rightarrow W
.
Let us denote by:

W^{\star}=W^{\times}_{n}\times W^{\times}_{n-1}\times\ldots\times W^{\times}_{1}\times W_{0}\ ,

(component
W_{0}

sic
).
The morphism (
17.9
) restricts to a morphism
m:\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\star}\rightarrow W
, which, by (
17.11
), is equivariant with respect to the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
.

We wish to define its quotient by
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
in the category of schemes.

Example 17.7
.

We warm up with the construction of projective space as a quotient.
Let
x_{i}^{1},\ldots,x_{i}^{n}
denote coordinates on the
k
-vector space
W_{i}\cong\mathbb{A}^{n}
, where
n=\dim W_{i}
, and write

W_{i}^{\times}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{n}U_{i}^{j}

where
U_{i}^{j}\subset\mathbb{A}^{n}
is the open complement of
x_{i}^{j}=0
. Let
U_{i}^{jk}=U_{i}^{j}\cap U_{i}^{k}
. The opens
U_{i}^{j},U_{i}^{jk}
are stable under the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
on
W
, and the inclusions
U_{i}^{jk}\hookrightarrow U_{i}^{j}
are
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. One may define the quotient by
\mathbb{G}_{m}
:

\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus W_{i}^{\times}:=\bigcup_{i=1}^{n}\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus U_{i}^{j}

to be the union of schemes
\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus U_{i}^{i}
glued along the
\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus U_{i}^{jk}
,
where
\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus U_{i}^{j}=\mathrm{Spec}\,(\mathcal{O}(U^{j}_{i})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}})
(and similarly
\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus U_{i}^{jk}
) is the affine scheme whose coordinate ring consists of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant polynomials. Since

\mathcal{O}(U_{i}^{j})^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}=k[\frac{x^{1}_{i}}{x_{i}^{j}},\ldots,\frac{x_{i}^{n}}{x_{i}^{j}}]
, we recover the standard affine covering of projective space and thus

\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus W_{i}^{\times}\cong\mathbb{P}(W_{i})

is canonically isomorphic to the projective space of
W_{i}
.

Definition 17.8
.

Consider a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant covering
W^{\times}_{i}=\bigcup_{1\leq j\leq\dim W_{i}}U_{i}^{j}
of
W_{i}
for every
i=1,\ldots,n
as in the previous example, and define a scheme:

(17.12)

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\star}):=\bigcup_{i_{1},\ldots,i_{n}}\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times U_{n}^{i_{n}}\times\ldots\times U_{1}^{i_{1}}\times W_{0}\right)\ ,

where each
1\leq i_{k}\leq\dim W_{k}
. It does not depend on the choice of coordinates (since
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
is central in the product of general linear groups
\mathrm{GL}(W_{i})
).

Since it is
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
-equivariant, the map
m
(
17.9
) defines a morphism of schemes also denoted by

(17.13)

m\ :\ \mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\star})\longrightarrow W\ .

Proposition 17.9
.

The scheme (
17.12
) canonically embeds as an open subscheme of the iterated blow-up
B\rightarrow W
of the affine space
W
along the linear subspaces

W_{0}\ \subset\ W_{0}\oplus W_{1}\ \subset\ \ldots\ \subset\ W_{0}\oplus W_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus W_{n-1}

in increasing order of dimension. It is Zariski-dense in
B
.

Proof.

The most direct proof is via explicit coordinates generalising example
17.7
.
Consider the coordinate ring of a particular open in (
17.12
)

\mathcal{O}\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times U_{n}^{i_{n}}\times\ldots\times U_{1}^{i_{1}}\times W_{0}\right)

where
1\leq i_{r}\leq\dim W_{r}
. With the labelling of coordinates of example
17.7
, it is

k\left[\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n},\left(x^{i}_{j}\right)_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq j\leq n\\
1\leq i\leq\dim W_{i}\end{subarray}},\frac{1}{x_{1}^{i_{1}}},\ldots,\frac{1}{x_{n}^{i_{n}}}\right]

where
\lambda_{i}
is the coordinate on the
i
th component of
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
(and the
x^{j}_{0}
are coordinates on
W_{0}
). The action of the
i
th component of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is equivalent to the action

\lambda_{j}\mapsto\begin{cases}\lambda_{j}\mu_{i}^{-1}\quad\hbox{ if }i=j\ ,\\
\lambda_{j}\quad\qquad\hbox{ if }i\neq j\end{cases}\quad\hbox{ and }\quad x_{k}^{j}\mapsto\begin{cases}\mu_{i}x_{k}^{j}\quad\hbox{ if }k\geq i\\
x_{k}^{j}\qquad\hbox{ if }k<i\end{cases}

for all
j\geq 1
. The ring of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
-invariants of
\mathcal{O}\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times U_{n}^{i_{n}}\times\ldots\times U_{1}^{i_{1}}\times W_{0}\right)
is

\mathcal{O}(W_{0})\Bigg[\left(\frac{x^{i}_{1}}{x^{i_{1}}_{1}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq\dim W_{1}},\left(\frac{x^{i}_{2}}{x^{i_{2}}_{2}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq\dim W_{2}},\ldots,\left(\frac{x^{i}_{n}}{x^{i_{n}}_{n}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq\dim W_{n}},\lambda_{1}x_{1}^{i_{1}},\lambda_{2}\frac{x_{2}^{i_{2}}}{x_{1}^{i_{1}}},\ldots,\lambda_{n}\frac{x_{n}^{i_{n}}}{x_{n-1}^{i_{n-1}}}\Bigg]\ ,

since the former is generated over the latter (which is clearly
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
-invariant) by
(x_{1}^{i_{1}})^{\pm 1},\ldots,(x_{n}^{i_{n}})^{\pm 1}
.
By introducing
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
-invariant variables

\alpha_{j}^{i}=\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}\ldots\lambda_{j}x^{i}_{j}

we can rewrite the ring of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
-invariants in the slightly different form

\mathcal{O}(W_{0})\Bigg[\alpha_{1}^{i_{1}},\left(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{i}}{\alpha_{1}^{i_{1}}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq\dim W_{1}},\frac{\alpha_{2}^{i_{2}}}{\alpha_{1}^{i_{1}}},\left(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{i}}{\alpha_{2}^{i_{2}}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq\dim W_{2}},\ldots,\quad\frac{\alpha_{n}^{i_{n}}}{\alpha_{n-1}^{i_{n-1}}},\left(\frac{\alpha_{n}^{i}}{\alpha_{n}^{i_{n}}}\right)_{1\leq i\leq\dim W_{n}}\Bigg]\ .

These are identical to a system of local coordinates on the iterated blow-up described in §
5.2
(after reversing the indexation of the subscripts of the
\alpha
’s) along

V(\alpha_{j}^{i}:j\geq 1)\quad\subset\quad V(\alpha_{j}^{i}:j\geq 2)\quad\subset\quad\ldots\quad\subset\quad V(\alpha_{j}^{i}:j\geq n)\ .

These coordinates define a canonical open immersion of this affine chart of (
17.12
) into
B
. Since these coordinate charts are compatible they glue together to form an explicit open immersion of schemes from (
17.12
) to
B
. ∎

Inspection of the proof shows that the scheme (
17.12
) is canonically isomorphic to the complement in
B
of strict transforms of some linear subspaces of
W
which may be written in terms of the
W_{i}
, although we will not need such an explicit description here.

Remark 17.10
.

The recursive structure of the iterated blow-up may be interpreted using the monoidal action.
Consider the morphism

(17.14)

\pi_{r}:\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r}\times W_{n}\times\ldots\times W_{r+1}\times W_{r}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{1}^{\times}\times W_{0}\longrightarrow\\
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r-1}\times W_{n}\times\ldots\times W_{r}\times W_{r-1}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{1}^{\times}\times W_{0}

which sends
((\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{r}),(w_{n},\ldots,w_{0}))
to
((\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{r-1}),(\lambda_{r}w_{n},\ldots,\lambda_{r}w_{r},w_{r-1},\ldots,w_{0}))
.

Lemma 17.11
.

The morphism
\pi_{r}
induces a map

(17.15)

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r}\times W_{n}\times\ldots\times W_{r+1}\times W_{r}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{1}^{\times}\times W_{0}\right)\longrightarrow\\
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r-1}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r-1}\times W_{n}\times\ldots\times W_{r}\times W_{r-1}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{1}^{\times}\times W_{0}\right)

which sends
V(\lambda_{r})
, the vanishing locus of
\lambda_{r}
, to

Z_{r}=0\times\ldots\times 0\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r-1}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{r-1}\times(W_{r-1}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{1}^{\times}\times W_{0})\right)\ .

Its restriction to the complement of
V(\lambda_{r})
is an isomorphism onto the complement of
Z_{r}
in its image. (It is not surjective, since the locus
w_{r}=0
is not in its image).

Proof.

The map
\pi_{r}
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant for the action of the last component of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r-1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
(with coordinate
\mu_{r}
)
and equivariant for the action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r-1}
(with coordinates
(\mu_{1},\ldots,\mu_{r-1})
).
Since
w_{r}\neq 0
, the equation
\lambda_{r}w_{r}\neq 0
is equivalent to
\lambda_{r}\neq 0
. Hence
\pi^{-1}_{r}(Z_{r})=V(\lambda_{r})
.
Recall that the last copy of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
in
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{r-1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
, with coordinate
\mu_{r}
, acts via

\mu_{r}\times(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{r},w_{n},\ldots,w_{0})\mapsto(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{r-1},\lambda_{r}\mu_{r}^{-1},\mu_{r}w_{n},\ldots,\mu_{r}w_{r},w_{r-1},\ldots,w_{0})

By passing to the quotient for this action, every point with
\lambda_{r}\neq 0
has a unique representative with
\lambda_{r}=1
. On such points, the map in question is the identity and hence
\pi_{r}
is an isomorphism onto its image outside
V(\lambda_{r})
.
∎

Now observe that the map
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\star})\rightarrow W
is a composition of morphisms

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\pi_{n}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle W_{n}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n-1}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n-1}\times(W_{n-1}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{1}^{\times}\times W_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\mathrm{id}\times\pi_{n-1}}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle W_{n}\times W_{n-1}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n-2}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n-2}\times(W_{n-2}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{1}^{\times}\times W_{0})\right)

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle W_{n}\times W_{n-1}\times\ldots W_{1}\times W_{0}\ .

The divisors
V(\lambda_{r})
can be described by a computation given in §
17.3.5
.
It follows from this and lemma
17.11
, that each map is a blow-up along
Z_{r}
, and one deduces that
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\star})
is an iterated blow-up along the strict transforms of
0\times\ldots\times 0\times W_{r-1}\times\ldots\times W_{0}
.

17.3.4.
Variant: projective version

With notation as above, let

W^{\circ}=W^{\times}_{n}\times W^{\times}_{n-1}\times\ldots\times W^{\times}_{1}\times W_{0}^{\times}

with an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
where
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
acts as above, but with an additional copy of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
(with coordinate
\mu_{0}
) acting by scalar multiplication on
W^{\circ}
(i.e. diagonally on each copy
W^{\times}_{i}
).
Then we may define the quotient
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\circ})
as before.
By (
17.10
), all components of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n}
act trivially on
W_{0}
and the morphism (
17.13
) now corresponds to:

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\circ})\longrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus(W\backslash\{0\})=\mathbb{P}(W)\ .

Note that its image is the open complement of
\mathbb{P}(W_{n}\oplus\ldots\oplus W_{1}\oplus\{0\})
in
\mathbb{P}(W)
.
The projective version of proposition
17.9
is:

Proposition 17.12
.

The scheme
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\circ})
, relative to the morphism:

\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\circ})\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(W)

canonically embeds into the iterated blow-up
B_{W}\rightarrow\mathbb{P}(W)
along the linear subspaces

\mathbb{P}(W_{0})\ \subset\ \mathbb{P}(W_{0}\oplus W_{1})\ \subset\ \ldots\ \subset\ \mathbb{P}(W_{0}\oplus W_{1}\oplus\ldots\oplus W_{n-1})

in increasing order of dimension. It is Zariski-dense in
B_{W}
.

17.3.5.
Exceptional divisors

The exceptional divisors of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\circ})
are in one-to-one correspondence with the divisors
V(\lambda_{i})
defined by
\lambda_{i}=0
for
i=1,\ldots,n
.

Lemma 17.13
.

The divisor
V(\lambda_{i})
is canonically isomorphic to a product

V(\lambda_{i})\ \cong\ \mathbb{G}_{m}^{n-i+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n-i}\times W_{n}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{i}^{\times})\ \times\ \mathbb{G}_{m}^{i}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{i-1}\times W_{i-1}^{\times}\times\ldots\times W_{0}^{\times})\ .

Proof.

There is a canonical isomorphism from

\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{i-1}\times 0\times\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n-i}\times W^{\times}_{n}\times\ldots\times W_{0}^{\times}
to

\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{i-1}\times W^{\times}_{i-1}\times\ldots\times W_{0}^{\times}\times\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n-i}\times W^{\times}_{n}\times\ldots\times W_{i}^{\times}

which maps an element
(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},0,\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_{n},w_{n},\ldots,w_{0})
to

(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},w_{i-1},\ldots,w_{0})\times(\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_{n},w_{n},\ldots,w_{i+1},w_{i})\ .

One checks that it is equivariant with respect to the respective actions of

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{m}^{i}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n-i+1}

\displaystyle\overset{\sim}{\longrightarrow}

\displaystyle\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}

\displaystyle(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{i-1})\times(\mu_{i},\ldots,\mu_{n})

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{i-1},\left(\mu_{0}\ldots\mu_{i-1}\right)^{-1}\mu_{i},\mu_{i+1},\ldots,\mu_{n})\ .

The statement follows by passing to the quotient.
∎

If we write
W^{\prime}=W_{i-1}\oplus\ldots\oplus W_{0}
, it follows that
V(\lambda_{i})
embeds into the product

(17.16)

V(\lambda_{i})\ \hookrightarrow\ B_{W/W^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{P}(W/W^{\prime})\times B_{W^{\prime}}\,\mathbb{P}(W^{\prime})

where the blow-ups are with respect to the flags of subspaces defined by the filtration
F_{m}=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{m}W_{k}
on
W
and the filtrations it induces on
W^{\prime}
and
W/W^{\prime}
.

The irreducible components
\mathcal{S}_{i}
of the exceptional divisor
\mathcal{S}
defines a stratification on the blow-up
B_{W}
of
\mathbb{P}(W)
. Consider the corresponding quasi-projective stratification

(17.17)

B_{W}=\bigcup_{I}\mathcal{S}^{\circ}_{I}

where the union is over all subsets
I\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
, and where we denote
\mathcal{S}_{I}=\bigcap_{i\in I}\mathcal{S}_{i}
, and
\mathcal{S}_{\emptyset}=\mathbb{P}(W)
, and define

\mathcal{S}^{\circ}_{I}=\mathcal{S}_{I}\setminus\left(\bigcup_{j\notin I}\mathcal{S}_{I}\cap\mathcal{S}_{j}\right)\ .

It follows from the description in proposition
5.3
that each stratum of (
17.17
) (and each stratum of
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\circ})
via (
17.16
)), is embedded in a product of projective spaces:

(17.18)

\mathcal{S}^{\circ}_{I}\quad\subset\quad\prod^{|I|}_{k=1}\mathbb{P}\left(\bigoplus_{i_{k-1}\leq j<i_{k}}W_{j}\right)=\prod^{|I|}_{k=1}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathrm{gr}^{F^{\prime}}_{k}W\right)\ ,

where
F^{\prime}W
is the increasing filtration on
W
whose
k^{\mathrm{th}}
graded quotient is

\bigoplus_{i_{k-1}\leq j<i_{k}}W_{j}
where we set
i_{0}=0,i_{n+1}=n+1
. The strata of (
17.17
) are in one-to-one correspondence with filtrations
F^{\prime}\leq F
.

The restriction of the embedding (
17.18
) to the locus in
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{n}\times W^{\circ})
where
\lambda_{i}=0
for all
i\in I
and

\lambda_{j}\neq 0
for all
j\neq 0
, is given explicitly by the map

(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{n},w_{n},\ldots,w_{0})\mapsto(w_{0},\ldots,w_{i_{1}-1})\times(w_{i_{1}},\ldots,w_{i_{2}-1})\times\ldots\times(w_{i_{n-1}},\ldots,w_{n})

where the right-hand side is in the space

\prod_{k=1}^{n+1}\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus(W^{\times}_{i_{k-1}}\times\ldots\times W_{i_{k}-1}^{\times})
which embeds in a product of projective spaces by example
17.7
. One may verify that it is an embedding because, for every
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
, we may apply a unique element of the
j
th copy of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
in
\mathbb{G}_{m}^{n+1}
to make the coordinate
\lambda_{j}
equal to
1
.

17.4.
Spaces of parabolic and symmetric matrices

Using the previous construction, we may define an algebraic version of
A_{P}\setminus(\overline{A}_{P}\times P)
associated to a rational parabolic
P
and embed it in an iterated blow-up. We then provide an algebraic interpretation of the space
X(P)
by embedding it into the real points of a finite iterated blow-up of projective space.
We shall denote algebraic groups with caligraphic letters to distinguish them from the topological groups considered in §
17.2
.

Let
V
be a finite-dimensional vector space over
\mathbb{Q}
and consider a flag of subspaces

F\ :\ 0\subset V_{d}\subset V_{d-1}\subset\ldots\subset V_{1}\subset V_{0}=V

which has a splitting:
V\cong\bigoplus_{i\geq 0}V_{i}/V_{i+1}
. Let

\mathcal{P}_{F}\subset\mathrm{End}_{F}(V)

denote the affine variety over
\mathbb{Q}
of endomorphisms of
V
which preserve
F
. Using the splitting of
V
we may write

\mathrm{End}_{F}(V)=\mathrm{Hom}_{F}(V\ ,\ \bigoplus_{i}V_{i}/V_{i+1})=\bigoplus_{i}\mathrm{Hom}_{F}(V,V_{i}/V_{i+1})

which, upon setting
\mathcal{P}_{i}=\mathrm{Hom}_{F}(V,V_{i}/V_{i+1})
, gives a decomposition (of schemes, corresponding to the direct sum in the category of vector spaces)

(17.19)

\mathcal{P}_{F}=\mathcal{P}_{d}\times\mathcal{P}_{d-1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{P}_{0}\ .

Define a subscheme
\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}=\mathcal{P}^{\times}_{d}\times\mathcal{P}^{\times}_{d-1}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{P}^{\times}_{0}
of
\mathcal{P}_{F}
as in §
17.3.4
.
Let

\mathcal{Z}_{P}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{d+1}\ \hookrightarrow\ \mathrm{GL}\left(\bigoplus_{i}V_{i}/V_{i+1}\right)=\prod_{i}\mathrm{GL}(V_{i}/V_{i+1})

be the product of multiplicative groups whose
k^{\mathrm{th}}
component, where
0\leq k\leq d
, acts by scalar multiplication on
V_{k}/V_{k+1}
. Denote its coordinate ring
\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Z}_{P})=k[\mu_{0},\mu_{0}^{-1},\ldots,\mu_{d},\mu_{d}^{-1}]
.

Consider the affine group scheme
\mathcal{A}_{P}=\mathbb{G}_{m}^{d}
, with coordinates
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d}
. There is a morphism of affine group schemes

\mathcal{A}_{P}\rightarrow\mathcal{Z}_{P}
,
which on coordinate rings is the map

(\mu_{0},\ldots,\mu_{d})\mapsto(1,\ \lambda_{1},\ \lambda_{1}\lambda_{2},\ \ldots,\ \lambda_{1}\cdots\lambda_{d}):\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{Z}_{P})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(\mathcal{A}_{P})\ .

This is consistent with (
17.7
). The previous morphism, via the action of
\mathcal{Z}_{P}
on
\bigoplus_{i}V_{i}/V_{i+1}
, defines an action of
\mathcal{A}_{P}
on

\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}
. This action extends to an action of the multiplicative monoid

\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}=\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{d}
whose
k^{\mathrm{th}}
component has coordinate
\lambda_{k}
where
1\leq k\leq d
.
It acts by scalar multiplication on
V_{k}

as in §
17.3
, i.e., for
v_{i}\in V_{i}/V_{i+1}
, for
0\leq i\leq d
we have:

(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d})\times(v_{d},\ldots,v_{1},v_{0})\mapsto(\lambda_{d}\cdots\lambda_{1}v_{d},\ \ldots,\ \lambda_{2}\lambda_{1}v_{2},\ \lambda_{1}v_{1},\ v_{0})\ .

Since the components of
\mathcal{Z}_{P}
act centrally on
V_{i}/V_{i+1}
, this defines an action
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\times\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}\rightarrow\mathcal{P}_{F}
by scalar multiplication on each component. It is given by the identical formula to the above, upon replacing
v_{i}
with
p_{i}
, for
p_{i}\in\mathcal{P}^{\times}_{i}
. On points, an element
a\in\mathcal{A}_{P}
acts upon
(\lambda,p)\in\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\times\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}
by
a.(\lambda,p)=(\lambda.a^{-1},a.p)
.
Finally let
\mathcal{H}=\mathbb{G}_{m}
denote the subgroup of
\mathcal{Z}_{P}
with coordinates
\mu_{0}=\ldots=\mu_{d}
, corresponding to diagonal matrices. It acts on
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\times\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}
by acting trivially on
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}
, and acting via scalar multiplication on

\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}
.

Theorem 17.14
.

There is a canonical Zariski-dense embedding:

(17.20)

(\mathcal{A}_{P}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}\right)\longrightarrow B_{F}\,\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})\ ,

where
B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})
is the iterated blow-up of
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})
along the subspaces

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0})\ \subset\ \mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{1})\ \subset\ \ldots\ \subset\ \mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{P}_{d-1})\ .

Let
I=\{i_{1},\ldots,i_{k}\}
and
1\leq i_{1}<\ldots<i_{k}\leq d
. The image of the locus
V(\lambda_{i},i\in I)
under the morphism (
17.20
) is contained in
the codimension
k
stratum
\mathcal{S}_{I}
of the exceptional divisor of
B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})
which corresponds to the flag
F^{\prime}\leq F
:

(17.21)

F^{\prime}\ :\ 0=V_{i_{k+1}}\subset V_{i_{k}}\subset V_{i_{k-1}}\subset\ldots\subset V_{i_{1}}\subset V_{i_{0}}=V\ .

Both
V(\lambda_{i},i\in I)
and its Zariski-closure
\mathcal{S}_{I}
are canonically isomorphic to products, and the morphism
(
17.20
) restricts to the embedding:

(17.22)

V(\lambda_{i},i\in I)\cong\prod_{m=0}^{k}(\mathcal{A}_{P_{F_{m}}}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P_{F_{m}}}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F_{m}}\right)\longrightarrow\mathcal{S}_{I}\cong\prod_{m=0}^{k}B_{F_{m}}\mathbb{P}(P_{F_{m}})

where
\mathcal{P}_{F_{m}}=\prod_{i_{m}\leq j<i_{m+1}}\mathcal{P}_{j}
,
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F_{m}}=\prod_{i_{m}\leq j<i_{m+1}}\mathcal{P}^{\times}_{j}
and

F_{m}
are the filtrations induced by
F
on the quotients
V_{i_{m}}/V_{i_{m+1}}
.

The restriction of (
17.22
) to the open locus
\{\lambda_{j}\neq 0:j\notin I\}
gives an embedding

(17.23)

(\mathcal{A}_{P_{F^{\prime}}}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}\quad\hookrightarrow\quad\mathcal{S}^{\circ}_{I}\subseteq\prod_{m=0}^{k}\mathbb{P}(P_{F_{m}})\ .

Proof.

Apply proposition
17.12
to
\mathcal{P}_{F}
. The second part follows by iterating the description of a codimension one exceptional divisor given in §
17.3.5
. The only novelty is (
17.23
).
It follows from the fact that the locus
\{\lambda_{j}\neq 0:j\notin I\}
is isomorphic to

\left(\prod_{m=0}^{k}(\mathcal{A}_{P_{F_{m}}}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P_{F_{m}}}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F_{m}}\right)\right)\setminus\bigcup_{j\notin I}V(\lambda_{j})\cong(\mathcal{A}_{P_{F^{\prime}}}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}\ .

This follows from the isomorphism
\mathcal{P}_{F}^{\circ}=\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F_{0}}\times\ldots\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F_{m}}
, and the
fact that the locus where
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
is a torsor over the jth copy of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
in
\mathcal{A}_{P_{F_{m}}}
, and so any point with coordinate
\lambda_{j}\neq 0
has a unique representative in its
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-orbit with
\lambda_{j}=1
.
∎

Since the determinant is a homogeneous polynomial in matrix entries, we may define the determinant hypersurface

\mathrm{Det}\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})
to be its vanishing locus. Let

\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\quad\subset\quad B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})

denote its strict transform.
Let
P=\mathrm{GL}_{F}(V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{R})
denote the (topological) group of automorphisms considered in §
17.2
, and likewise
A_{P}\leq P
.
Then we may interpret

P\ \cong\ \left(\mathcal{P}_{F}\backslash\mathrm{Det}\right)(\mathbb{R})

and it follows from theorem
17.14
that there are injections:

(17.24)

(A_{P}\times H)\setminus\left(\overline{A}_{P}\times P\right)\longrightarrow\left((\mathcal{A}_{P}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\times(\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}\backslash\mathrm{Det}\right)\right)(\mathbb{R})\longrightarrow\left(B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})\setminus\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})\ .

On the left is a topological group; in the middle, the real points of a scheme-theoretic group quotient; on the right, the real points of an iterated blow up.

Example 17.15
.

Consider the case when
d=2
and
F
is
0\subset V_{2}\subset V_{1}\subset V
. By choosing a basis of
V
adapted to this filtration,
the points of
\mathcal{P}_{F}
are given by block lower triangular matrices, and
\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\cong\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{2}
acts by

(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\times\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}&&\\
P_{01}&P_{1}&\\
P_{02}&P_{12}&P_{2}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}&&\\
\lambda_{1}P_{01}&\lambda_{1}P_{1}&\\
\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}P_{02}&\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}P_{12}&\lambda_{1}\lambda_{2}P_{2}\end{pmatrix}

where
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}
are points on
\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{2}
.
The points of
\mathcal{P}_{i}
, for
i=0,1,2
are given by the subsets of matrices which vanish everywhere except in (block) row
i+1
, and the subspaces
\mathcal{P}_{0}\subset\mathcal{P}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{1}
which are to be blown up are the spaces of matrices of the form:

\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}\ \subseteq\ \begin{pmatrix}P_{0}&0&0\\
P_{01}&P_{1}&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}\ .

The points of the subspace
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}
satisfy
P_{0}\neq 0
,
P_{1}\neq 0
and
P_{2}\neq 0
. This strictly contains the complement of the locus
\mathrm{Det}

defined by
\det(P_{0})\det(P_{1})\det(P_{2})=0
.

The stratification of
\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}^{2}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F})
is generated by the equations
\lambda_{1}=0
and
\lambda_{2}=0
(below left). The strata themselves are described as quotients (below, middle), which embed into the following products of blow-ups (below, right):

\begin{array}[]{rccc}V(\lambda_{1})\quad:&\left(\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{0}\right)\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{1}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{2}\right)&\subseteq&\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0})\times B_{\,\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{1})}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{2}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{1})\\
V(\lambda_{2})\quad:&\mathbb{G}_{m}^{2}\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{0}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{1}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{2}\right)&\subseteq&B_{\,\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0})}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{1})\times\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{2})\\
V(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\quad:&\left(\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{0}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{1}\right)\times\left(\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{2}\right)&\subseteq&\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0})\times\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{1})\times\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{2})\ .\end{array}\ .

These strata, respectively, correspond to the flags:

0\subset V_{1}\subset V
for
\lambda_{1}=0
;
0\subset V_{2}\subset V
for
\lambda_{2}=0
; and the full flag
0\subset V_{2}\subset V_{1}\subset V
for
\lambda_{1}=\lambda_{2}=0
.

See Example
17.17
for the continuation of this example.

17.5.
Spaces of quadratic forms, blow-ups and
X(P)

Let
F
,
P
be as in §
17.4
. There is a natural morphism of algebraic varieties

(17.25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})

\displaystyle\longrightarrow

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))

\displaystyle M

\displaystyle\mapsto

\displaystyle M^{T}M

which sends the subspace

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{0}\oplus\ldots\oplus\mathcal{P}_{i})
to
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i+1}))\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
, the projective space of the subspace of quadratic forms which vanish on
V_{i+1}
.

Consequently, by the universal property of blow-ups, (
17.25
) induces a morphism

(17.26)

B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})\longrightarrow B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))

where

B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
is the iterated blow-up of
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
along the linear subspaces

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{1})\right)\ \subset\ \mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{2})\right)\ \subset\ \ldots\ \subset\ \mathbb{P}\left(\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{d})\right)

associated to the flag
F
.
Combining with theorem
17.14
we deduce a natural map:

(17.27)

(\mathcal{A}_{P}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}\right)\longrightarrow B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})\longrightarrow B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))\ .

Recall from §
9.5
that the exceptional divisor
\mathcal{E}
of
B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
defines a stratification
whose strata
\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}
are in one-to-one correspondence with flags
F^{\prime}\leq F
(
17.21
), and
are canonically isomorphic to a product of iterated blow-ups

\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\cong\prod_{m=0}^{k}B_{F_{i}}\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m+1}})}{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m}})}\right)\ ,\

where
F_{i}
is the induced filtration on the successive quotients
V_{i}/V_{i+1}
of (
17.21
).
Let
\mathrm{Det}\subset\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
denote the vanishing locus of the determinant, and
\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}
its strict transform in
B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
.
It was proven in proposition
11.4
that

(17.28)

\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\setminus\left(\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)\cong\prod_{m=0}^{k}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m+1}})}{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m}})}\right)\ \setminus\ \mathrm{Det}\big|_{V_{i_{m}}/V_{i_{m+1}}}\right)\

where
\mathrm{Det}\big|_{V_{i_{m}}/V_{i_{m+1}}}
is the zero locus of the homogeneous map

\frac{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m+1}})}{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m}})}\longrightarrow\mathcal{Q}(V_{i_{m}}/V_{i_{m+1}})\overset{\mathrm{det}}{\longrightarrow}\mathbb{Q},

where the first map is restriction of quadratic forms (compare §
9.3
).

Theorem 17.16
.

The map (
17.27
) induces an injective continuous map

(17.29)

X(P)\longrightarrow\left(B_{F}\,\mathbb{P}(Q(V))\ \setminus\ \widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})\ .

For every flag
F^{\prime}
of the form (
17.21
) the map (
17.29
) restricts to a map

(17.30)

e(P_{F^{\prime}})\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\setminus\left(\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\cap\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})

to the corresponding stratum in the exceptional locus of
B_{F}\,\mathbb{P}(Q(V))
.
The image of
e(P_{F^{\prime}})
, via the identification (
17.28
), is the product
e(P_{F^{\prime}})\cong\prod_{m}X_{m}
where

X_{m}\ \subset\ \mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m+1}})}{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{i_{m}})}\right)(\mathbb{R})

is the set of projective classes of matrices whose image in

\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V_{i_{m}}/V_{i_{m+1}}))(\mathbb{R})
is positive definite.

Proof.

The equation
\det(M^{T}M)=\det(M)^{2}
implies that (
17.25
) preserves the determinant loci
\mathrm{Det}
in
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{P}_{F})
and
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
, and so the morphism (
17.26
) preserves their strict transforms.
It follows that (
17.27
) defines a natural map

(17.31)

(\mathcal{A}_{P}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\left(\overline{\mathcal{A}}_{P}\times\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}\right)\longrightarrow B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))\setminus\ \widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}

whose restriction to the open locus
\{\lambda_{i}\neq 0,\hbox{ for }1\leq i\leq d\}
is the morphism

\mathbb{G}_{m}\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))\setminus\mathrm{Det}

which, in coordinates given by a basis of
V
, sends a matrix
M
to the projective class of
M^{T}M
. This map is invariant by left-multiplication by the subscheme

\mathcal{K}_{P_{F}}\leq\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}

whose points are matrices
O
such that
O^{T}O=1
, since one has
(OM)^{T}OM=M
.
Since this equation is algebraic, it remains true on the Zariski closure of
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}
, and hence (
17.31
) is also invariant under left multiplication by
\mathcal{K}_{P_{F}}
, since the action of
\mathcal{K}_{P_{F}}
on
\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}
commutes with that of
\mathcal{A}_{P}
and
\mathcal{H}
.
We deduce that the map of topological spaces

(A_{P}\times H)\setminus\left(\overline{A}_{P}\times P\right)\longrightarrow\left(B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))\setminus\ \widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})

which is induced by taking real points of the map

(
17.31
)
and composing with the first map in (
17.24
), is
K_{P}\leq\mathcal{K}_{P_{F}}(\mathbb{R})
invariant and so passes to the quotient

X(P)=Z_{P}K_{P}\setminus\left(\overline{A}_{P}\times P\right)\longrightarrow\left(B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))\setminus\ \widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})\ .

It gives a well-defined continuous map (
17.29
) such that the following diagram commutes:

\begin{array}[]{ccc}Z_{P}\setminus\left(\overline{A}_{P}\times P\right)&\longrightarrow&\left(B_{F}\mathbb{P}(P_{F})\setminus\ \widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})\\
\downarrow&&\downarrow\\
X(P)=A_{P}\setminus\left(\overline{A}_{P}\times X\right)&\longrightarrow&\left(B_{F}\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))\setminus\ \widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})\end{array}\ ,

where the horizontal map along the top is the morphism (
17.20
) and
the vertical maps are
M\mapsto M^{T}M
on the left, and (
17.26
) on the right.

Now compute the restriction of the map (
17.29
) on each stratum indexed by a flag
F^{\prime}
of the form (
17.21
).
The map (
17.31
) restricts to a map on exceptional divisors

(\mathcal{A}_{P_{F^{\prime}}}\times\mathcal{H})\setminus\!\setminus\mathcal{P}^{\circ}_{F}\ \overset{\eqref{OpenStratumAndAPquotient}}{\longrightarrow}\ \mathcal{S}^{\circ}_{F^{\prime}}\overset{\eqref{blowupMTM}}{\longrightarrow}\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\ .

Since it is left-invariant by
\mathcal{K}_{P_{F}}
, it descends, after taking real points, to the quotient

e(P_{F^{\prime}})=A_{P_{F^{\prime}}}\setminus X\cong Z_{P_{F^{\prime}}}K_{P_{F^{\prime}}}\setminus P_{F^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\backslash\left(\mathcal{E}_{F^{\prime}}\setminus\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}\right)(\mathbb{R})\ .

This morphism is injective on each stratum
e(P_{F^{\prime}})
since
M^{T}M=N^{T}N
holds for two invertible real matrices
M,N
if and only if
M=ON
, for
O
an orthogonal matrix.
The final statement follows from the fact that (
17.29
) identifies the open
X\subset X(P)
with the subspace of projective classes of positive-definite matrices inside
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))(\mathbb{R})
. This proves the result for the trivial flag. In the case of a flag
0\subset V_{1}\subset V
of length
d=1
, the map from
e(P)
to the corresponding exceptional locus is given on the level of matrices by

(17.32)

\begin{pmatrix}A&0\\
U&B\end{pmatrix}\quad\mapsto\quad\begin{pmatrix}A^{T}A&0\\
0&0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}0&U^{T}B\\
B^{T}U&B^{T}B\end{pmatrix}\ ,

where the matrices are on the right denote projective equivalence classes. We can choose
A,B
such that
A^{T}A
, and
B^{T}B
are arbitrary (projective classes of) positive definite real symmetric matrices, and finally choose
U
such that
U^{T}B
is any other matrix, since
B
is invertible. This indeed proves that
e(P)
surjects onto the space described in the statement of the theorem. The general case, for a flag of arbitrary length, is similar (or follows from the case of length one by induction).
∎

Examples 17.17
.

As in example
17.15
,
use the splitting
V\cong V/V_{1}\oplus V_{1}/V_{2}\oplus V_{2}
to write endomorphisms of
V
in block matrix form.
Consider the deepest stratum which is associated to the full flag
F
, which corresponds to
\mathcal{E}_{12}
(denoted
V(\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
in example
17.15
)).

The morphism (
17.30
)

e(P)\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{1}))(\mathbb{R})\times\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{2})}{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{1})}\right)(\mathbb{R})\times\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathcal{Q}(V)}{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{2})}\right)(\mathbb{R})

is given explicitly on matrix representatives in
P
of classes in
e(P)=A_{P}K_{P}\setminus P
by:

\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}&&\\
P_{01}&P_{1}&\\
P_{02}&P_{12}&P_{2}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}^{T}P_{0}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,\ \begin{pmatrix}0&P_{01}^{T}P_{1}&0\\
P_{1}^{T}P_{01}&P_{1}^{T}P_{1}&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,\ \begin{pmatrix}0&0&P_{02}^{T}P_{2}\\
0&0&P_{12}^{T}P_{2}\\
P_{2}^{T}P_{02}&P_{2}^{T}P_{12}&P_{2}^{T}P_{2}\end{pmatrix}

Now consider the stratum associated to the flag
F^{\prime}:0\subset V_{1}\subset V
, which corresponds to the exceptional divisor
\mathcal{E}_{1}
(corresponding to
V(\lambda_{1})
in example
17.15
). The morphism (
17.30
)

e(P^{\prime})\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{1}))(\mathbb{R})\times\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{\mathcal{Q}(V)}{\mathcal{Q}(V/V_{1})}\right)(\mathbb{R})

is given on matrix representatives in
P
of classes in
e(P^{\prime})=A_{P^{\prime}}K_{P^{\prime}}\setminus P^{\prime}=A_{P^{\prime}}K_{P}\setminus P
by
setting
A=P_{0}
,
U=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P_{01}\\
P_{02}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
, and
B=\left(\begin{smallmatrix}P_{1}&0\\
P_{12}&P_{2}\end{smallmatrix}\right)
in (
17.32
). This gives the map

\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}&&\\
P_{01}&P_{1}&\\
P_{02}&P_{12}&P_{2}\end{pmatrix}\mapsto\begin{pmatrix}P_{0}^{T}P_{0}&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix}\ ,\ \begin{pmatrix}0&P_{01}^{T}P_{1}+P_{02}^{T}P_{12}&P_{02}^{T}P_{2}\\
P_{1}^{T}P_{01}+P_{12}^{T}P_{02}&P_{1}^{T}P_{1}+P_{12}^{T}P_{12}&P_{12}^{T}P_{2}\\
P_{2}^{T}P_{02}&P_{2}^{T}P_{12}&P_{2}^{T}P_{2}\end{pmatrix}

17.6.
Proof of the statements in §
17.1

17.6.1.
Proof of theorem
17.2

Theorem
17.16
provides an injective map of stratified spaces from
X(P)
to the iterated blow-up
B_{F}\mathbb{P}(Q(V))
.
By the argument of Proposition
5.2
(ii), there is a canonical map
\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{R})\rightarrow B_{F}\mathbb{P}(Q(V))
which is an isomorphism away from exceptional divisors. Since the image of the exceptional divisors is contained in the (strict transform) of the determinant locus, which does not meet the image of
X(P)
, it follows that the continuous map
X(P)\rightarrow B_{F}\mathbb{P}(Q(V))\setminus\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}}
canonically lifts to a map

X(P)\rightarrow(\mathfrak{B}\setminus\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}})(\mathbb{R})
. Via the description (
17.8
) of
X^{\mathrm{BS}}
as a union of
X(P)
, for
P
rational parabolics, we deduce the canonical continous map
X^{\mathrm{BS}}\rightarrow(\mathfrak{B}\setminus\widetilde{\mathrm{Det}})(\mathbb{R})
. It is injective since, by Theorem
17.16
, the image of each open stratum
e(P)
of
X^{\mathrm{BS}}
is contained in the complement of the determinant locus in a unique exceptional stratum. They are disjoint, since the intersections of exceptional strata are contained in the determinant loci.
The statement abut the closure of
X
will follow from the argument in the next paragraph below.
The description of the image of
e(P)
follows from Theorem
17.16
and (
17.28
).
Finally, the group of rational points
\mathrm{GL}(V)(\mathbb{Q})
acts on
X
via
P\mapsto Pg
. Correspondingly, the
algebraic group
\mathrm{GL}(V)
acts upon
\mathbb{P}(\mathcal{Q}(V))
via
(g,M)\mapsto g^{T}Mg
. Since it permutes the set of flags (
17.5
) and hence the centers of all the blow-ups which define
\mathfrak{B}
, it extends to a natural action of
\mathrm{GL}(V)
upon
\mathfrak{B}
. Its group of
\mathbb{Q}
-points
\mathrm{GL}(V)(\mathbb{Q})
acts algebraically upon
\mathfrak{B}
. This action is by compatible, via
P\mapsto P^{T}P
, with the action of
\mathrm{GL}(V)(\mathbb{Q})
upon
X
.

17.6.2.
Proof of corollary
17.3

Consider a
\mathrm{GL}_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
-equivariant admissible decomposition
X=\bigcup_{\sigma}\sigma
, obtained, for example, as the intersection of an admissible decomposition §
10.3
with the set of positive definite symmetric matrices.
Let us identify
X^{\mathrm{BS}}
with its image in
\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{R})
under the map
f
. Thus we may write

X\subset X^{\mathrm{BS}}\subset\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{R})\ .

Consider a polyhedron
\sigma\subset X
, and denote its closure in
X^{\mathrm{BS}}
by
\overline{\sigma}
. Its closure in
\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{R})
is the compact polyhedron

\sigma^{\mathcal{B}}=\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{\min,\sigma}}
in the minimal blow-up of
\sigma
, since, by definition, the latter is the topological closure of the inverse image of the interior of
\sigma
in its minimal blow up. The equality
\sigma^{\mathcal{B}}=\sigma^{\mathcal{B}^{\min,\sigma}}
follows from invariance under extraneous modifications (proposition
5.14
).
By proposition
11.4
, each open face of
\sigma^{\mathcal{B}}
is a product of strictly positive polyhedra, and is therefore contained in the image of an
e(P)
, for a certain
P
, by the last part of theorem
17.16
. It follows that
\sigma^{\mathcal{B}}\subset X^{\mathrm{BS}}
. Thus

X\subset\bigcup_{\sigma}\sigma^{\mathcal{B}}\subset X^{\mathrm{BS}}\

and in particular
\overline{\sigma}=\sigma^{\mathrm{BS}}
.
By construction,
X(P)=A_{P}\setminus(\overline{A}_{P}\times X)
is contained in the closure of
X=A_{P}\setminus(A_{P}\times X)
(let the coordinates
\lambda_{i}
on
A_{P}
tend to zero) and so
X^{\mathrm{BS}}
is contained in the closure of
X
. The previous equation then implies that

(17.33)

X^{\mathrm{BS}}=\bigcup_{\sigma}\sigma^{\mathcal{B}}

It follows that
X^{\mathrm{BS}}
equals the closure of
X
in
\mathfrak{B}(\mathbb{R})
, which was the remaining statement to prove in theorem
17.2
.
The decomposition (
17.33
) has similar properties to an admissible decomposition as in §
10.3
(finitely many
\mathrm{GL}_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
orbits, intersections are closures of faces, etc). Conclude using the fact that, by definition (
11.5
), the topological space
\left|L\mathcal{A}_{g}^{\mathrm{trop},\mathfrak{B}}\right|
is the quotient of
\bigcup_{\sigma}\sigma^{\mathcal{B}}
by the action of
\mathrm{GL}_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
, since the gluing relations in the diagram category
\mathcal{D}_{g}^{\mathrm{trop},\mathcal{B}}
are those induced by
\mathrm{GL}_{g}(\mathbb{Z})
.
